[01:29:58.000 -> 01:40:02.000] The speakers discuss the killing of a young man named Haidar from Kafr Masr by Jews near Kawkab, the flight of families (Dar Abu Hussein, Dar Fayeq) due to fear of occupation, the ultimate departure of the villagers to Thaljaboul and Khirbet Kharbush, shortages of food (eating barley bread) during the war, and hearing news of the Deir Yassin massacre while still in the village.
[01:39:58.000 -> 01:50:02.000] The speakers discuss the events of fleeing the village of Kawkab during the war, including hiding in caves and rough terrain (al-wa'r), attacks by planes and tanks, shooting at fleeing civilians, leaving belongings like bedding and a full pantry behind, and the displacement of the entire village.
[01:49:58.000 -> 02:00:02.000] The speaker describes leaving their village out of fear during the war, the involvement of the Iraqi army, crossing the river (Shari'a) while water was flooded, living in tents in various locations (Taybeh, Tamra, Samma, Manshiyya), selling livestock to survive, and receiving UNRWA rations.
[01:59:58.000 -> 02:10:02.000] She describes visiting her demolished village and expresses heartbreak (hasra) saying 'where did we live and where are we now', and states 'the country is gone from us'.
[00:00:00.000 -> 00:05:10.000] بعد النكبة (after the Nakba); نتيجة النكبة (result of the Nakba); التاريخ الشفوي للنكبة الفلسطينية (Oral history of the Palestinian Nakba)
[02:19:58.000 -> 02:30:02.000] The speakers discuss the flight from the village of Kawkab during the war. The interviewee describes the fear preventing them from farming, the news of the Deir Yassin massacre prompting fear, aerial bombardment by planes, and the final departure where they left all belongings and provisions behind. She details hiding in caves and the wilderness (al-Wa'ar), the separation of families, and leaving behind an elderly blind man.
[02:29:58.000 -> 02:40:02.000] The speaker describes fleeing her village due to fear, crossing the Shari'a (Jordan River) on trucks or tractors, and becoming refugees in Samma and Manshiyya. She recounts leaving an elderly relative behind, who was later taken to Taybeh. She details the conditions of displacement, living in tents and huts, and the death of her son.
[02:39:58.000 -> 02:50:02.000] The interviewer asks the speaker if she visited her country 'after the Nakba', and she confirms she did.
[02:49:58.000 -> 02:56:05.091] المحاور يذكر قرار الأمم المتحدة رقم 194 الذي أقر حق العودة والتعويض عن المعاناة التي لحقت باللاجئين نتيجة النكبة.
